<p><b>Wallis and Futuna</b>, officially the <b>Territory of the Wallis and Futuna Islands</b><sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;w&#39; in &#39;wind&#39;">w</span><span title="/ɒ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;body&#39;">ɒ</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="/ɪ/: &#39;i&#39; in &#39;kit&#39;">ɪ</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span></span>&#8201;...<span class="wrap">&#8201;</span><span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;f&#39; in &#39;find&#39;">f</span><span title="/uː/: &#39;oo&#39; in &#39;goose&#39;">uː</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;t&#39; in &#39;tie&#39;">t</span><span title="/uː/: &#39;oo&#39; in &#39;goose&#39;">uː</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>/</a></span></span>), is a <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">French</a> island <a href="/wiki/Territorial_collectivity" title="Territorial collectivity">collectivity</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Oceania" title="Oceania">South Pacific</a>, situated between <a href="/wiki/Tuvalu" title="Tuvalu">Tuvalu</a> to the northwest, <a href="/wiki/Fiji" title="Fiji">Fiji</a> to the southwest, <a href="/wiki/Tonga" title="Tonga">Tonga</a> to the southeast, <a href="/wiki/Samoa" title="Samoa">Samoa</a> to the east, and <a href="/wiki/Tokelau" title="Tokelau">Tokelau</a> to the northeast.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Mata_Utu" title="Mata Utu">Mata Utu</a> is its capital and largest city. Its land area is 142.42&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (54.99&#160;sq&#160;mi). It had a population of 11,558 at the 2018 census (down from 14,944 at the 2003 census).<sup id="cite_ref-census_2018_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census_2018-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-past_censuses_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-past_censuses-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> The territory is made up of three main <a href="/wiki/Volcano" title="Volcano">volcanic</a> <a href="/wiki/Tropical_island" class="mw-redirect" title="Tropical island">tropical islands</a> and a number of tiny <a href="/wiki/Islets" class="mw-redirect" title="Islets">islets</a>. It is divided into two island groups that lie about 260&#160;km (160&#160;mi) apart: the <a href="/wiki/Wallis_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Wallis Island">Wallis Islands</a> (also known as <a href="/wiki/Uvea_(Wallis_and_Futuna)" title="Uvea (Wallis and Futuna)">Uvea</a>) in the northeast; and the <a href="/wiki/Hoorn_Islands" title="Hoorn Islands">Hoorn Islands</a> (also known as the Futuna Islands) in the southwest, including <a href="/wiki/Futuna_Island,_Wallis_and_Futuna" class="mw-redirect" title="Futuna Island, Wallis and Futuna">Futuna Island</a> proper and the mostly uninhabited <a href="/wiki/Alofi_Island" title="Alofi Island">Alofi Island</a>.
</p><p>Since 28 March 2003, Wallis and Futuna has been a <a href="/wiki/French_overseas_departments_and_territories#Overseas_collectivities" class="mw-redirect" title="French overseas departments and territories">French overseas collectivity</a> (<i><a href="/wiki/Collectivit%C3%A9_d%27outre-mer" class="mw-redirect" title="Collectivité d&#39;outre-mer">collectivité d'outre-mer</a></i>, or <i>COM</i>).<sup id="cite_ref-Wallis-et-Futuna_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Wallis-et-Futuna-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> Between 1961 and 2003, it had the status of a French overseas territory (<i><a href="/wiki/Territoire_d%27outre-mer" class="mw-redirect" title="Territoire d&#39;outre-mer">territoire d'outre-mer</a></i>, or <i>TOM</i>). Its official name did not change when its status changed.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_humans">Early humans</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Early humans">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The earliest signs of human habitation on these islands are artifacts characteristic of the <a href="/wiki/Lapita_culture" title="Lapita culture">Lapita culture</a>, dating roughly to between 850 and 800 BCE. The islands served as natural stopover points for boat traffic, mostly between <a href="/wiki/Fiji" title="Fiji">Fiji</a> and <a href="/wiki/Samoa" title="Samoa">Samoa</a>. During Tongan invasions in the 15th and 16th centuries, the islands defended themselves with varying levels of resistance, but also accepted varying degrees of assimilation. Futuna retained more of its pre-<a href="/wiki/Tongan_culture" class="mw-redirect" title="Tongan culture">Tongan cultural features</a>, while Wallis underwent greater fundamental changes in its society, language, and culture.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> The original inhabitants built forts and other identifiable structures on the islands (many of which are in ruins), some of which are still partially intact. Oral history and archaeological evidence suggests that the Tongan invaders re-occupied and modified some of these structures. The oral history also preserves a cultural memory of relationships between Samoa and Futuna that are so longstanding, they are described in the islanders' origin stories.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="European_settlements">European settlements</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: European settlements">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Futuna was first put on European maps by <a href="/wiki/Willem_Schouten" title="Willem Schouten">Willem Schouten</a> and <a href="/wiki/Jacob_Le_Maire" title="Jacob Le Maire">Jacob Le Maire</a>, during their <a href="/wiki/Circumnavigation" title="Circumnavigation">circumnavigation</a> of the globe, in 1616. They named the islands of Futuna "Hoornse Eylanden", after the Dutch town of <a href="/wiki/Hoorn" title="Hoorn">Hoorn</a> where they hailed from. This was later translated into French, as "Isles de Horne." The Wallis Islands are named after the British explorer <a href="/wiki/Samuel_Wallis" title="Samuel Wallis">Samuel Wallis</a>, who sailed past them in 1767, after being the first European to visit <a href="/wiki/Tahiti" title="Tahiti">Tahiti</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> The French were the first Europeans to settle in the territory,<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> with the arrival of French <a href="/wiki/Missionary" title="Missionary">missionaries</a> in 1837, who converted the population to Roman Catholicism. <a href="/wiki/Peter_Chanel" title="Peter Chanel">Pierre Chanel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonized</a> in 1954, is a major <a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">patron saint</a> of the island of Futuna and of the region.
</p><p>On 5 April 1842, the missionaries asked for the protection of France, after the uprising of part of the local population. On 5 April 1887, the queen of <a href="/wiki/Uvea_(Wallis_and_Futuna)" title="Uvea (Wallis and Futuna)">Uvea</a> (of the traditional <a href="/wiki/Chiefdom" title="Chiefdom">chiefdom</a> of Wallis) signed a treaty, officially establishing a French <a href="/wiki/Protectorate" title="Protectorate">protectorate</a>. The kings of <a href="/wiki/Sigave" title="Sigave">Sigave</a> and <a href="/wiki/Alo_(Wallis_and_Futuna)" title="Alo (Wallis and Futuna)">Alo</a> (on the islands of Futuna and <a href="/wiki/Alofi" title="Alofi">Alofi</a>) also signed a treaty establishing a French protectorate, on 16 February 1888. From that moment, the islands were officially under the authority of the <a href="/wiki/French_colonial_empire" title="French colonial empire">French colony</a> of <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia" title="New Caledonia">New Caledonia</a>.
</p><p>In 1917, the three traditional kingdoms of Uvea, Sigave, and Alo were <a href="/wiki/Annexation" title="Annexation">annexed</a> by France, integrated into the colony of Wallis and Futuna, and remained under the authority of the colony of New Caledonia.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="World_War_II">World War II</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: World War II">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

<p>During <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, the islands' administration was briefly pro-<a href="/wiki/Vichy_France" title="Vichy France">Vichy</a>, until a <i>Free French</i> <a href="/wiki/Corvette" title="Corvette">corvette</a> from New Caledonia deposed the regime, on 26 May 1942. Units of the <a href="/wiki/US_Marine_Corps" class="mw-redirect" title="US Marine Corps">US Marine Corps</a> later landed on Wallis, on 29 May 1942.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Overseas_territory">Overseas territory</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Overseas territory">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1959, the inhabitants of the islands <a href="/wiki/1959_Wallis_and_Futuna_status_referendum" title="1959 Wallis and Futuna status referendum">voted</a> to become a separate <a href="/wiki/Overseas_departments_and_territories_of_France" class="mw-redirect" title="Overseas departments and territories of France">French overseas territory</a>, effective since 29 July 1961,<sup id="cite_ref-Wallis-et-Futuna_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Wallis-et-Futuna-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> thus ending their subordination to New Caledonia.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2005, the 50th <a href="/wiki/Lavelua" class="mw-redirect" title="Lavelua">king of Uvea</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tomasi_Kulimoetoke_II" title="Tomasi Kulimoetoke II">Tomasi Kulimoetoke II</a>, faced being deposed after giving sanctuary to his grandson who was convicted of manslaughter. The king claimed his grandson should be judged by tribal law rather than by the French penal system. As a result, there were riots in the streets involving the king's supporters, who were victorious over attempts to replace the king. Two years later, Tomasi Kulimoetoke died on 7 May 2007. The state was in a six-month period of mourning, during which mentioning a successor was forbidden.<sup id="cite_ref-BBC_News_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC_News-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> On 25 July 2008, <a href="/wiki/Kapiliele_Faupala" class="mw-redirect" title="Kapiliele Faupala">Kapiliele Faupala</a> was installed as king despite protests from some of the royal clans. He was deposed in 2014. A new king, <a href="/wiki/Patalione_Kanimoa" title="Patalione Kanimoa">Patalione Kanimoa</a>, was eventually installed in Uvea in 2016; Lino Leleivai in Alo on Futuna succeeded after Filipo Katoa had abdicated, and Eufenio Takala succeeded Polikalepo Kolivai in Sigave. The French president at the time, <a href="/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Hollande" title="François Hollande">François Hollande</a>, attended the installation ceremony.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Governance_and_law">Governance and law</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Governance and law">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Wallis_and_Futuna" title="Politics of Wallis and Futuna">Politics of Wallis and Futuna</a></div>
<p>The territory is divided into three traditional kingdoms (<i>royaumes coutumiers</i>): <a href="/wiki/Uvea_(Wallis_and_Futuna)" title="Uvea (Wallis and Futuna)">Uvea</a>, on the island of Wallis, <a href="/wiki/Sigave" title="Sigave">Sigave</a>, on the western part of the island of Futuna, and <a href="/wiki/Alo_(Wallis_and_Futuna)" title="Alo (Wallis and Futuna)">Alo</a>, on the eastern part of the island of Futuna and on the uninhabited island of Alofi (only Uvea is further subdivided, into three districts):

</li>
</ol></div></div>
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The capital of the collectivity is <a href="/wiki/Mata_Utu" title="Mata Utu">Mata Utu</a> on the island of Uvéa, the most populous of the Wallis Islands. As an overseas collectivity of France, it is governed under the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_France" title="Constitution of France">French constitution</a> of 28 September 1958, and has universal <a href="/wiki/Suffrage" title="Suffrage">suffrage</a> for those over 18 years of age. The <a href="/wiki/President_of_France" title="President of France">French president</a> is elected by popular vote for a five-year term; the high administrator is appointed by the French president on the advice of the <a href="/wiki/French_Ministry_of_the_Interior" class="mw-redirect" title="French Ministry of the Interior">French Ministry of the Interior</a>; the presidents of the Territorial Government and the Territorial Assembly are elected by the members of the assembly. The <a href="/wiki/2022_Wallis_and_Futuna_Territorial_Assembly_election" title="2022 Wallis and Futuna Territorial Assembly election">most recent election was held on 20 March 2022</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2021, the head of state is President <a href="/wiki/Emmanuel_Macron" title="Emmanuel Macron">Emmanuel Macron</a> of France, as represented by Administrator-Superior Thierry Queffelec.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> The president of the Territorial Assembly has been Petelo Hanisi since 11 December 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> The Council of the Territory consists of three kings (monarchs of the three pre-colonial kingdoms) and three members appointed by the high administrator on the advice of the Territorial Assembly.
</p><p>The legislative branch consists of the unicameral 20-member Territorial Assembly or <i><a href="/wiki/Assembl%C3%A9e_territoriale" class="mw-redirect" title="Assemblée territoriale">Assemblée territoriale</a></i>. Its members are elected by popular vote, and serve five-year terms. Wallis and Futuna elects one senator to the <a href="/wiki/French_Senate" class="mw-redirect" title="French Senate">French Senate</a> and one deputy to the <a href="/wiki/French_National_Assembly" class="mw-redirect" title="French National Assembly">French National Assembly</a>.
</p><p>Criminal justice is generally governed by French law and administered by a tribunal of first resort in Mata Utu; appeals from that tribunal are decided by the <a href="/wiki/Court_of_Appeal_(France)" class="mw-redirect" title="Court of Appeal (France)">Court of Appeal</a> in <a href="/wiki/Noum%C3%A9a" title="Nouméa">Nouméa</a>, <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia" title="New Caledonia">New Caledonia</a>. However, in non-criminal cases (civil-law disputes), the three traditional kingdoms administer justice according to <a href="/wiki/Customary_law" title="Customary law">customary law</a>.
</p><p>The territory participates in the <a href="/wiki/CFP_franc" title="CFP franc">Franc Zone</a>, and is both a permanent member of the <a href="/wiki/Secretariat_of_the_Pacific_Community" class="mw-redirect" title="Secretariat of the Pacific Community">Secretariat of the Pacific Community</a> and an observer at the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Islands_Forum" title="Pacific Islands Forum">Pacific Islands Forum</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography_and_climate">Geography and climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Geography and climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Wallis_and_Futuna_in_France_(zoomed).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Wallis_and_Futuna_in_France_%28zoomed%29.svg/220px-Wallis_and_Futuna_in_France_%28zoomed%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Wallis_and_Futuna_in_France_%28zoomed%29.svg/330px-Wallis_and_Futuna_in_France_%28zoomed%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Wallis_and_Futuna_in_France_%28zoomed%29.svg/440px-Wallis_and_Futuna_in_France_%28zoomed%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="4842" data-file-height="2724" /></a><figcaption>Location map of Wallis and Futuna in relation to France.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Wallis_and_Futuna-CIA_WFB_Map.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Wallis_and_Futuna-CIA_WFB_Map.png/220px-Wallis_and_Futuna-CIA_WFB_Map.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="236" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/f4/Wallis_and_Futuna-CIA_WFB_Map.png 1.5x" data-file-width="330" data-file-height="354" /></a><figcaption>Map of the territory of Wallis and Futuna</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Wallis_vue_du_ciel.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Wallis_vue_du_ciel.jpg/220px-Wallis_vue_du_ciel.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Wallis_vue_du_ciel.jpg/330px-Wallis_vue_du_ciel.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Wallis_vue_du_ciel.jpg/440px-Wallis_vue_du_ciel.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5184" data-file-height="3456" /></a><figcaption>Aerial view of <a href="/wiki/Wallis_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Wallis Island">Wallis Island</a> (Uvea)</figcaption></figure>
<p>Wallis and Futuna is located about two-thirds of the way from Hawaii to New Zealand. It is <span class="geo-inline"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1156832818"><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;params=13_18_S_176_12_W_type:isle"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">13°18′S</span> <span class="longitude">176°12′W</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">13.300°S 176.200°W</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">-13.300; -176.200</span></span></span></a></span></span>, 360&#160;km (220&#160;mi) west of Samoa and 480&#160;km (300&#160;mi) northeast of Fiji.
</p><p>The territory includes the island of Uvéa (also called Wallis), which is the most populous; the island of Futuna; the virtually uninhabited island of Alofi; and 20 uninhabited islets. The total area of the territory is 274 square kilometres (106&#160;sq&#160;mi), with 129 kilometres (80&#160;mi) of coastline. The highest point in the territory is <a href="/wiki/Mont_Puke" title="Mont Puke">Mont Puke</a>, on the island of Futuna, at 524 metres (1,719&#160;ft).
</p><p>The islands have a hot, rainy season from November to April, when tropical cyclones passing over them cause storms. Then they have a cool, dry season from May to October, caused by the southeast trade winds that predominate during those months. The average annual rainfall is between 2,500 and 3,000 millimetres (98–118&#160; in), and rain is likely on at least 260 days per year. The average humidity is 80%. The average annual temperature is 26.6&#160;°C (79.9&#160;°F), rarely falling below 24.0&#160;°C (75.2&#160;°F); during the rainy season, it ranges between 28.0&#160;°C (82.4&#160;°F) and 32.0&#160;°C (89.6&#160;°F).
</p><p>Only 5% of the islands' land area consists of <a href="/wiki/Arable_land" title="Arable land">arable land</a>; permanent crops cover another 20%. <a href="/wiki/Deforestation" title="Deforestation">Deforestation</a> is a serious problem: Only small portions of the original forests remain, largely because the inhabitants use wood as their main fuel source, and, as a result, the mountainous terrain of Futuna is particularly susceptible to <a href="/wiki/Erosion" title="Erosion">erosion</a>. The island of Alofi lacks natural freshwater resources, so it has no permanent settlements.
</p><p>Volcanic activity during the mid-<a href="/wiki/Pleistocene" title="Pleistocene">Pleistocene</a> created numerous <a href="/wiki/Volcanic_crater_lake" title="Volcanic crater lake">volcanic crater lakes</a> on <a href="/wiki/Uvea_(Wallis_and_Futuna)" title="Uvea (Wallis and Futuna)">Uvea</a> (Wallis Island). The names of some of them are: <a href="/wiki/Lake_Lalolalo" title="Lake Lalolalo">Lalolalo</a>, Lano, <a href="/wiki/Lake_Lanutavake" title="Lake Lanutavake">Lanutavake</a>, Lanutuli, Lanumaha, <a href="/wiki/Lake_Kikila" title="Lake Kikila">Kikila</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Lake_Alofivai" title="Lake Alofivai">Alofivai</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Wallis and Futuna is part of the <a href="/wiki/Fiji_tropical_moist_forests" title="Fiji tropical moist forests">Fiji tropical moist forests</a> terrestrial ecoregion.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-21">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Flora_and_fauna">Flora and fauna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Flora and fauna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_mammals_of_the_Wallis_and_Futuna_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="List of mammals of the Wallis and Futuna Islands">List of mammals of the Wallis and Futuna Islands</a></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Wallis_and_Futuna" title="Economy of Wallis and Futuna">Economy of Wallis and Futuna</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Gross_domestic_product" title="Gross domestic product">GDP</a> of Wallis and Futuna in 2005 was US$188&#160;million (at market exchange rates).<sup id="cite_ref-GDP_2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GDP-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> 
The territory's economy consists mostly of traditional subsistence agriculture, with about 80% of the labor force earning its livelihood from agriculture (<a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">coconuts</a> and vegetables), livestock (mostly pigs), and fishing. About 4% of the population is employed in government. Additional revenue comes from French government subsidies, licensing of fishing rights to Japan and South Korea, import taxes, and remittances from expatriate workers in New Caledonia, French Polynesia, and France. Industries include <a href="/wiki/Copra" title="Copra">copra</a>, handicrafts, fishing, and lumber. Agricultural products include coconuts, <a href="/wiki/Breadfruit" title="Breadfruit">breadfruit</a>, yams, taro, bananas, pigs, and fish.
Exports include copra, chemicals, and fish.
</p><p>There is a single bank in the territory, Banque de Wallis-et-Futuna, established in 1991. It is a subsidiary of <a href="/wiki/BNP_Paribas" title="BNP Paribas">BNP Paribas</a>. There had previously been a branch of <a href="/wiki/Banque_Indosuez" title="Banque Indosuez">Banque Indosuez</a> at Mata Utu. It had opened in 1977, but was closed in 1989, leaving the territory without any bank for two years.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Population">Population</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Population">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Wallis_and_Futuna" title="Demographics of Wallis and Futuna">Demographics of Wallis and Futuna</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Taoa_(Futuna).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Taoa_%28Futuna%29.jpg/220px-Taoa_%28Futuna%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="160" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Taoa_%28Futuna%29.jpg/330px-Taoa_%28Futuna%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Taoa_%28Futuna%29.jpg/440px-Taoa_%28Futuna%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3518" data-file-height="2552" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Futuna_(Wallis_and_Futuna)" title="Futuna (Wallis and Futuna)">Futuna Island</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:S%C3%A9r%C3%A9nit%C3%A9_-_plage_d%27Alofi_(Wallis_et_Futuna).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/S%C3%A9r%C3%A9nit%C3%A9_-_plage_d%27Alofi_%28Wallis_et_Futuna%29.jpg/220px-S%C3%A9r%C3%A9nit%C3%A9_-_plage_d%27Alofi_%28Wallis_et_Futuna%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/S%C3%A9r%C3%A9nit%C3%A9_-_plage_d%27Alofi_%28Wallis_et_Futuna%29.jpg/330px-S%C3%A9r%C3%A9nit%C3%A9_-_plage_d%27Alofi_%28Wallis_et_Futuna%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/S%C3%A9r%C3%A9nit%C3%A9_-_plage_d%27Alofi_%28Wallis_et_Futuna%29.jpg/440px-S%C3%A9r%C3%A9nit%C3%A9_-_plage_d%27Alofi_%28Wallis_et_Futuna%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5184" data-file-height="3456" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Alofi_Island" title="Alofi Island">Alofi Island</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The total population of the territory at the July 2018 census was 11,558 (72.1% on the island of Wallis, 27.9% on the island of Futuna),<sup id="cite_ref-census_2018_1-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census_2018-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> down from 14,944 at the July 2003 census.<sup id="cite_ref-past_censuses_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-past_censuses-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> The vast majority of the population are of <a href="/wiki/Polynesian_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Polynesian people">Polynesian</a> ethnicity, with a small minority who were born in <a href="/wiki/Metropolitan_France" title="Metropolitan France">Metropolitan France</a> or are of French descent.
</p><p>Lack of economic opportunities has, since the 1950s, been impelling many young Wallisians and Futunians to migrate to the more prosperous French territory of <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia" title="New Caledonia">New Caledonia</a>, where, as French citizens, they are legally entitled to settle and work. Since the mid-2000s, emigration has surged in response to political tensions on the main island of Wallis (Uvea), that have arisen from a feud between rival aristocratic clans, who are supporting competing kings. Emigrants have begun settling, not only in New Caledonia, but also much farther away, in Metropolitan France. At the 2019 New Caledonian census, 22,520 residents of New Caledonia (whether born in New Caledonia or in Wallis and Futuna) reported their ethnicity as "Wallisian and Futunian".<sup id="cite_ref-Population_Structure_of_Communities_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Population_Structure_of_Communities-25">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> This is almost double the total population of Wallis and Futuna.
</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Wallisian_language" title="Wallisian language">Wallisian language</a> and <a href="/wiki/Futunan_language" title="Futunan language">Futunan language</a></div>
<div class="PieChartTemplate thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px">
<div class="mw-no-invert" style="background-color:white;margin:auto;position:relative;width:200px;height:200px;overflow:hidden;border-radius:100px;border:1px solid black;transform:scaleX(-1)rotate(-90deg)">
<div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 5304.5364363156px; border-left-color:#FF33AC"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:#FF33AC"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:#FF33AC"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;left:100px; top:100px; border-width:100px 0 0 93.906250581749px; border-left-color:#36A"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:#36A"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 100px 200px 0;border-color:#36A"></div><div style="border:solid transparent;background-color: initial;position:absolute;width:100px;line-height:0;right:100px; top:100px; border-width:54.112125212688px 84.094458229817px 0 0; border-top-color:#6A5"></div>
<div style="position:absolute;line-height:0;border-style:solid;left:0;top:0;border-width:0 200px 100px 0;border-color:#6A5"></div>
</div>
<div class="thumbcaption">
<p><b>Languages spoken at home in Wallis and Futuna (2018 Census)<sup id="cite_ref-home_language_2018_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-home_language_2018-27">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup></b>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r981673959">.mw-parser-output .legend{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .legend-color{display:inline-block;min-width:1.25em;height:1.25em;line-height:1.25;margin:1px 0;text-align:center;border:1px solid black;background-color:transparent;color:black}.mw-parser-output .legend-text{}</style><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#6A5; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Wallisian (59.1%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#36A; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;Futunan (27.9%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#FF33AC; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;French (12.7%)</div>
</div>
</div></div>
<p>According to the 2018 census, among people 14 y/o and older, 59.1% reported <a href="/wiki/Wallisian" class="mw-redirect" title="Wallisian">Wallisian</a> as the language they speak the most at home (down from 60.2% in 2008), 27.9% reported <a href="/wiki/Futunan" class="mw-redirect" title="Futunan">Futunan</a> (down from 29.9% in 2008), and 12.7% reported French (up from 9.7% in 2008).<sup id="cite_ref-home_language_2018_27-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-home_language_2018-27">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-home_languages_2008_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-home_languages_2008-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> On <a href="/wiki/Wallis_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Wallis Island">Wallis Island</a>, the languages most spoken at home were Wallisian (82.2%, down from 86.1% in 2008), French (15.6%, up from 12.1% in 2008), and Futunan (1.9%, up from 1.5% in 2008).<sup id="cite_ref-home_language_2018_27-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-home_language_2018-27">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-home_languages_2008_28-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-home_languages_2008-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> On <a href="/wiki/Futuna_Island,_Wallis_and_Futuna" class="mw-redirect" title="Futuna Island, Wallis and Futuna">Futuna</a>, the languages most spoken at home were Futunan (94.5%, down from 94.9% in 2008), French (5.3%, up from 4.2% in 2008), and Wallisian (0.2%, down from 0.8% in 2008).<sup id="cite_ref-home_language_2018_27-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-home_language_2018-27">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-home_languages_2008_28-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-home_languages_2008-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At the 2018 census, 90.5% of people 14 y/o and older could speak, read and write either Wallisian or Futunan (up from 88.5% at the 2008 census), and 7.2% had no knowledge of either Wallisian or Futunan (same percentage as at the 2008 census).<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Among those 14 y/o and older, 84.2% could speak, read and write French in 2018 (up from 78.2% at the 2008 census), and 11.8% reported that they had no knowledge of French (down from 17.3% at the 2008 census).<sup id="cite_ref-french_language_2018_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-french_language_2018-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-french_language_2008_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-french_language_2008-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> On Wallis Island, 85.1% of people age 14 or older could speak, read and write French (up from 81.1% at the 2008 census), and 10.9% reported that they had no knowledge of French (down from 14.3% at the 2008 census).<sup id="cite_ref-french_language_2018_31-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-french_language_2018-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-french_language_2008_32-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-french_language_2008-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> On Futuna, 81.9% of people age 14 or older could speak, read and write French (up from 71.6% at the 2008 census), and 14.0% had no knowledge of French (down from 24.3% at the 2008 census).<sup id="cite_ref-french_language_2018_31-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-french_language_2018-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-french_language_2008_32-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-french_language_2008-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Cath%C3%A9drale_de_Mata-Utu_(Wallis-et-Futuna)_drone.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Cath%C3%A9drale_de_Mata-Utu_%28Wallis-et-Futuna%29_drone.png/220px-Cath%C3%A9drale_de_Mata-Utu_%28Wallis-et-Futuna%29_drone.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Cath%C3%A9drale_de_Mata-Utu_%28Wallis-et-Futuna%29_drone.png/330px-Cath%C3%A9drale_de_Mata-Utu_%28Wallis-et-Futuna%29_drone.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Cath%C3%A9drale_de_Mata-Utu_%28Wallis-et-Futuna%29_drone.png/440px-Cath%C3%A9drale_de_Mata-Utu_%28Wallis-et-Futuna%29_drone.png 2x" data-file-width="1920" data-file-height="1080" /></a><figcaption><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cathedral_of_Our_Lady_of_the_Assumption,_Mata_Utu&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Cathedral of Our Lady of the Assumption, Mata Utu (page does not exist)">Cathedral of Our Lady of the Assumption, Mata Utu</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The overwhelming majority (99%) of the people in Wallis and Futuna are <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholics</a>, with the island having been evangelized in the 19th century by Fr <a href="/wiki/Peter_Chanel" title="Peter Chanel">Peter Chanel</a>, SM.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-33">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> They are served by their own <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Wallis_and_Futuna" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Wallis and Futuna">Diocese of Wallis and Futuna</a>, with a see at <a href="/wiki/Mata_Utu" title="Mata Utu">Mata Utu</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Suffragan_diocese" title="Suffragan diocese">suffragan diocese</a> of the Metropolitan <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Noum%C3%A9a" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Nouméa">Archdiocese of Nouméa</a> (New Caledonia).<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Wallis_and_Futuna" title="Culture of Wallis and Futuna">Culture of Wallis and Futuna</a></div>
<p>The culture of Wallis and Futuna is <a href="/wiki/Polynesian_culture" title="Polynesian culture">Polynesian</a>, and is similar to the cultures of its neighbouring nations <a href="/wiki/Samoa" title="Samoa">Samoa</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tonga" title="Tonga">Tonga</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Wallisian" class="mw-redirect" title="Wallisian">Wallisian</a> and <a href="/wiki/Futunan" class="mw-redirect" title="Futunan">Futunan</a> cultures are very similar to each other in language, <a href="/wiki/Dance_of_Wallis_and_Futuna" title="Dance of Wallis and Futuna">dance</a>, cuisine and modes of celebration.
</p><p>Fishing and agriculture are the traditional occupations, and most people live in traditional oval, thatch <i>fale</i> houses.<sup id="cite_ref-Ibpus.comUSA2012_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ibpus.comUSA2012-35">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Kava" title="Kava">Kava</a> is a popular beverage brewed in the two islands, as in much else of Polynesia. It also serves as a traditional offering in rituals.<sup id="cite_ref-Ibpus.comUSA2012_35-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ibpus.comUSA2012-35">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> Highly detailed <a href="/wiki/Tapa_cloth" title="Tapa cloth">tapa cloth</a> art is a specialty of Wallis and Futuna.<sup id="cite_ref-HinzHoward2006_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HinzHoward2006-36">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Uvea_Museum_Association" title="Uvea Museum Association">Uvea Museum Association</a> is a private museum that holds a collection of objects that record the history of the Second World War in the territory.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-37">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> It is located in Mata Utu shopping centre and in 2009 was open by appointment.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport_and_communications">Transport and communications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Transport and communications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>In 2018, the territory had 3,132 telephones in use,<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> one AM radio station, and two television broadcast stations.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2016)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Communication costs are up to ten times higher<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2016)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> than in western countries. The island of Wallis has about 100 kilometres (62&#160;mi) of roadways, 16 of which are paved. The island of Futuna has only 20 kilometres (12&#160;mi) of roadways, none of which are paved. The territory has two main ports, in the harbours at Mata Utu and <a href="/wiki/Leava" title="Leava">Leava</a> (on the island of Futuna). These ports support its <a href="/wiki/Merchant_marine" class="mw-redirect" title="Merchant marine">merchant marine</a> fleet, which comprises three ships (two passenger ships and a petroleum tanker), totaling 92,060 GRT or 45,881 tonnes. There are two airports: <a href="/wiki/Hihifo_Airport" title="Hihifo Airport">Hihifo Airport</a>, on Wallis, which has a 2.1 kilometres (1.3&#160;mi) paved runway; and <a href="/wiki/Pointe_Vele_Airport" title="Pointe Vele Airport">Pointe Vele Airport</a>, on Futuna, which has a 1 kilometre (0.62&#160;mi) runway.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> Now the only commercial flights to and from Wallis are operated by Caledonia-based <a href="/wiki/Aircalin" title="Aircalin">Aircalin</a>, which has an office in Mata Utu. There are currently no commercial boat operators.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Newspapers">Newspapers</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Newspapers">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The French High Commission published a local newspaper in the 1970s, <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Nouvelles_de_Wallis_et_de_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Nouvelles de Wallis et de Futuna (page does not exist)">Nouvelles de Wallis et de Futuna</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> Today, news is available online via a local program broadcast by the French television network <a href="/wiki/La_Premi%C3%A8re_(French_TV_network)" title="La Première (French TV network)">La Première</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>There are 18 primary schools in the territory, 12 on Wallis and six on Futuna, with a combined total of over 5200 students.<sup id="cite_ref-Educ_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Educ-43">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The territory has six junior high schools and one senior high school/sixth-form college.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>Junior high schools (<i>collèges</i>) in Wallis: Mataotama de Malae, Alofivai de Lano, Vaimoana de Lavegahau, and Tinemui de Teesi</li>
<li>Junior high schools in Futuna: Fiua de Sigave and Sisia d'Ono</li>
<li>The senior high school/sixth-form college is <a href="/wiki/Lyc%C3%A9e_des_%C3%8Eles_Wallis_et_Futuna" title="Lycée des Îles Wallis et Futuna">Lycée d'État de Wallis et Futuna</a> on Wallis</li></ul>
<p>There is also an agricultural high school.<sup id="cite_ref-Educ_43-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Educ-43">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Healthcare">Healthcare</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Healthcare">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Wallis_and_Futuna" title="COVID-19 pandemic in Wallis and Futuna">COVID-19 pandemic in Wallis and Futuna</a></div>
<p>As of 2018, <a href="/wiki/Yaws" title="Yaws">yaws</a> was endemic in the area, but cases were not being reported to the WHO eradication programme.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Healthcare is available free of charge in two hospitals on Uvea and Futuna islands.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> There are also three dispensaries.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Environment">Environment</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Wallis_and_Futuna&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Environment">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Deforestation is a major concern in the region as only small portions of the original forests remain due to the continued use of wood as the main source of fuel. Consequently, the mountainous terrain of Futuna has become prone to erosion. There are no permanent settlements on Alofi due to the lack of natural freshwater resources, and the presence of infertile soil on the islands of Uvea and Futuna further reduces the agricultural productivity.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
